## Claude

The `art_sheet.py` reviewer found one real defect and fixed it. The claims in `052d4e8d05` held: no installed art is hidden, ordering and letters are unchanged, and the `art_sheet` selftests are 21/21 green.

- **Defect:** `_assign_letters` gave a remembered letter back by picture bytes only. When two lanes show the same pictures (a Gizka render and GizkaW's installed copy), the earlier lane's column could take the other lane's letter. `enact` installs a letter to the lane `graphic_of` names, so a pick could have gone to the wrong texture path. It now matches bytes plus lane first, then bytes alone. The new test returns `['B','C']` on the old code and `['C','B']` on the new.
- **Snapshots:** the all_bridge_art and drawbridge_art decisions files hold no human-written decisions, so no owner pick was lost. Every dropped row is one whose options are all ruled (protected), which the sheet drops by design.
- **Commits:** fix `1e8bdb8513`, ledger `877a44a64f`, both pushed. `ART_SHEET_REFTEX_FIX_UNREVIEWED` is closed at `1e8bdb8513`.
- **Not clean:** `art_sheet.py` stays DIRTY, because the reviewer changed the code and so couldn't mark it clean. It also only partly read the JS templates (lines ~1575-2316).
- **Two low findings,** filed as `ART_SHEET_REFTEX_STATUSNONE_CENSUS_LOSSY`:
  - References are never shown on rows whose in-game size has status "none" (UI-only defs), in either the Python or the JS.
  - The census file is rewritten without the dropped references even when the gate then refuses the sheet.

Each review so far has produced a fix that needs its own review. To break that chain, I started a final fresh full-file reviewer on `art_sheet.py` with the JS templates included, focused on pick → decisions file → `art.py enact` routing. It may edit the file only for a severe defect, such as a wrong texture installed or a lost owner pick. Otherwise it files each defect as its own item, and it records a fix-or-drop verdict on the two low findings. The guard-hardening helper is also still running.

## Claude

The final `art_sheet.py` reviewer found one severe defect and fixed it in commit `c3a382b9e0`.

- **Defect:** pressing a number key on a row (1-9 picks A-I on every row), or shift+number for a group, could record a set from another lane, or a letter with no set at all, as that row's main pick. `art.py enact` would then install those pictures at the row's main texture path, putting the wrong texture in.
- **Fix:** `setDecision` now refuses a letter that isn't a set of the row's main slot and shows an error. Clicking a picked letter again to clear it still works. The new test in `selftest_sheet_slots.py` fails 2 checks on the old code and passes now, and the sheet selftests pass 8/8.
- **Past picks:** the reviewer checked all 178 decisions files and found no pick that was ever misrouted this way.
- **Not clean:** `art_sheet.py` stays DIRTY, because the reviewer changed its code.
- **Items filed, each with a failing-test sketch:**
  - `SHEET_BULK_ENFORCE_NOBULK_FLAG`: the shared review-sheets template's shift+number still applies a valid letter to every row in a group.
  - `SHEET_SCHEMA1_FIX_MOVED_LETTER`: an old-format pick whose letter moved to another lane on a rebuilt sheet is routed by the new lane.
  - `ENACT_SLOT_PICK_VERIFY_MEMBERSHIP`: `enact` never checks that a pick's letter belongs to that slot.
  - `ART_SHEET_HOTKEY_GUARD_VET_UNREVIEWED`: a review of this fix.
- **Dropped:** `ART_SHEET_REFTEX_STATUSNONE_CENSUS_LOSSY`. Both findings only affect which reference pictures are shown and cannot lose or misroute a pick. A later note on the item corrects one claim in the drop reason that the reviewer had not checked row by row.
- **Closed:** `ART_SHEET_LANE_LETTERS_FIX_UNREVIEWED`, at `c3a382b9e0`.
- **Commits:** the fix `c3a382b9e0`, plus ledger sync and a lesson in `d460b2ff4b`, both pushed.

I started an Opus helper to vet that fix and to add the membership check to `enact` in `art.py`, a different file, so the review chain doesn't continue in `art_sheet.py`. It may edit `art_sheet.py` only for a severe defect, and it leaves its own `enact` change DIRTY for an independent review. ## Claude

Your answers on the three questions are recorded.

- **Shrink three, done with GPT:** Bloodlurk C, Scaldhide B and Ribbonwhip B get redrawn at 256 through GPT image editing, not a resize filter. The helper holds the creature, pose, facing, palette and silhouette fixed and checks each result against its original for drift. It judges each as a naive judge would, with up to 3 attempts per image. If GPT's version is worse than the original, nothing is installed and it comes back to you.
- **Earlier shrinks:** TunnelSnake, Vurra, Durrok and Surrik were shrunk with a resize filter before you said that. The helper judges them for mutilation, such as thin features lost or aliasing. It gives a recommendation per creature and redoes one only if it is clearly mutilated, and then only queues it.
- **ContagionIkee F:** the old keep is released through the art ledger's owner-ruling route, with your card as authority, and F is purged.
- **Fleshsop:** your yes means the purge stands. Nothing more to do.

The `enact.py` and series-stop reviewer found nothing installed at a wrong lane's path and no mixed-facing creature. That held only because helpers manually held Fuzzrunner C, BedBug V_E, TunnelSnake, Vurra and DecayDrake C. ## Claude

Sources for this walk-through are the builder's brief and the design note `design/Jawa/worldbuilding/world_faces_own_art_2026-10-23.md`. I haven't looked at the pictures myself, and neither has the judge for this version. The helper looked at every face file and close-up for the full-resolution rebuild, and the earlier pre-review used the seat plus a GPT judge.

**What you're deciding:** which look our own world-map faces get. World Map Enhanced stays out; every picture is our own, made by code with no donor bytes. The sheet has 4 looks × 4 shipping biomes (Pyrelands, LeaningScrub, Cauldron, Contagion), plus hills.

**Why:**
- **September:** it looked pretty because of light, clearly different colours per biome, and a small drawn mark on nearly every hex. The texture grain wasn't it.
- **Today's faces:** they were built to a "calm" spec copied from World Map Enhanced's texture statistics. In our rendering they land dim and low-contrast: Cauldron reads olive-brown, Contagion a flat crimson, and LeaningScrub an even olive.

**The four rows per biome:**
- **Today:** the installed calm faces.
- **A. Painted:** World Map Enhanced's technique in our colours. Domain-warped mottle in three close hues, soft crust patches and a faint crease net, with one tell per biome. It is textures only, with no code to install.
- **B. Relief:** a lit heightfield coloured by height. It is textures only, and the busiest at far zoom, where the repeat shows.
- **C. Clean field plus our own decals:** the September route.
  - It is a light, nearly flat face with 2-3 small drawn glyphs per tile (Pyrelands straw tufts and burn lines, LeaningScrub shrubs, Cauldron vent mounds, Contagion pustule clusters).
  - Glyphs are lit from the upper left and never rotate. They fade out past mid zoom, because at far zoom a decal becomes speckle.
  - It costs a new per-tile decal layer in `RM_WorldLook`, a Mod Settings toggle ("Planet: biome decals"), one glyph atlas per biome and an altitude fade.

**Hills, independent of the look:** the choice is the installed soft relief, or our own drawn low-poly mountain clumps. The clumps need our own hill pass, because vanilla's hill quads rotate at random and would spin the lighting.

**Pre-review and the agent's lean:**
- **GPT judge:** it preferred A for textures alone.
- **Judge flags:** painted Pyrelands smears repeated like stamps, and Cauldron was noisy (both quieted). Relief looked embossed, with Contagion "like minced meat". Shading was cut about 45% and it is still flagged busy. C's mountains read as game pieces, which was fixed.
- **Lean:** C, because the September charm is per-hex marks plus clear colour.
- **Prefill:** C = Pick, A = Maybe, B and Today = No, Hills 1 (installed soft relief) = Pick, Hills 2 (drawn clumps) = Maybe.

**Unmeasured, and why it matters:**
- **Scale:** how many hexes one face spans in game is unmeasured. The renderer assumes about 3, so the far-zoom repeat may look different live.
- **Edges:** September had hard hex edges against our soft borders, and a settings toggle exists, so trying both is a flip at the live look.
- **Decal fade:** whether the decal fade altitude matches the zoom where the white-speckle complaint arose is also unmeasured. That speckle on Rust Cathedral is actually Odyssey landmark icons, not faces.
- **Checker:** whichever look wins needs its own bounds written into the face checker at install, because all three new looks break the old calm bounds on purpose.

**What your pick does:** it is tried on the planet first. Nothing is installed until you choose. The chosen look then extends to every biome table, with palettes from each biome's sheet. The three owner-overridden ones keep their overrides: Abyss near-black with swirls, Rot with wild-colour swirls, and Rust Cathedral as harsh machinery.
